Nothing in this repository can publish today, so no headless process can trip it. Also measured by the planner and folded in: `go list -overlay` **SILENTLY IGNORES** a `Replace` key matching no file (rc=0, base closure, **no stderr**), so `AC2`'s "closure contains `mutantImport`" needed strengthening to the closure `checkGoGroup` actually **CONSUMED** plus a negative half (free — `checkGoGroup` already computes and discards it); and `git status --porcelain` reports **untracked** files, so `AC4`'s in-tree ready marker would have redded on the harness's own artifact (marker moved outside `repoRoot`, residue assertion path-scoped to the four targets). `AC3` and `AC5` are sound; `AC3`'s `:217–:222` citation was **never verified by the doc** and the controller verified it first-party (it is exactly the two RED-fidelity assertions, `:218` and `:221`); `AC5`'s baseline PASSES at HEAD (both named tests, rc=0). **MILESTONES: `BG.A` (AC2, AC3, AC4, AC5 · M1, M2, M4, M5) → `BG.B` (AC1a · M3, M6) → `BG.C` (AC1b, AC6′ · M7)** — partition COMPLETE, 7 criteria and 7 mutations, none dropped or double-assigned. ONE ordering joint is **forced** (the AST guard reds on sight while `mutateAndRestore` still calls `os.WriteFile` at `:205/:209/:224`, so `BG.B` cannot precede `BG.A`); one is **chosen** (`BG.C` last, because it is the only milestone whose green depends on an UNMEASURED CI-filesystem property, so a red there leaves the repair already landed). **CARRIED, NOT CLOSED — `C1` IS APFS-ONLY AND MAY NOT TRANSFER:** the `ModTime` backstop was measured 200/200 on darwin/APFS while CI job 2 is `ubuntu-latest`; **Linux takes mtime from a tick-granularity coarse clock (1–4 ms)**, and the planner could not measure it (no docker/colima/podman/lima on the rig) — so it is labelled UNMEASURED rather than assumed. Hence `BG.C` last, a **fail-loud 20/20 granularity probe whose failure is a TEST FAILURE naming both `st_dev`s**, sha256+size+mode+**inode** asserted unconditionally (inode closes a rename route that BOTH of the doc's stated observables miss), and a pre-authorized fallback: record the refutation, keep the four filesystem-independent observables, open `10/OD-3` — and **NEVER lower the 20/20 threshold**. **ESTIMATE HONESTLY SPLIT:** the doc's **≤1 day of EFFORT holds** (velocity derived by command — 12 landed feat/fix commits, insertions median **363**, mean 447; closest analogue `1761a9c`, a single-test-file change to *this same file*, +56/−7, one iteration) **but ELAPSED is 2–3 iterations**, because measured cadence is ≤1 milestone/iteration across iters 41–56 and **4 of the 7 mutations CANNOT run in the executor sandbox** (M5 needs subprocess SIGKILL + git inspection; M6/M7 re-arm live-tree writes; AC6′ needs a file swap out of git history) — **the controller pass is the critical path**, which is a routing fact, not a complaint. LOC re-estimated **+150 → +250**, itemised, all of it spent making the doc's own ACs non-vacuous. **A CHARTER DEFECT FOUND IN ITER-56'S OWN CORRECTION, AND IT IS THE SAME SHAPE AS EVERYTHING ELSE HERE.** iter-56 measured *"deliberately non-compiling"* FALSE and stamped *"Corrected in all three places this Gate 4."* Verified rather than inherited: the **dashboard** was genuinely corrected (1 hit, and it is the correction itself), but the charter's **item-10 queue row still carried the false sentence in LIVE prose ~35 lines BELOW its own correction** — `~~` marker count **2** in that span proves one closed strikethrough covering only the `[BACKLOG …]` tag, so the sentence was never struck. **A correction that does not delete the text it corrects leaves a reader able to find the false version, and the loudness of the correction is what makes nobody re-read the row.** Fixed this Gate 4. The iter-55 STATUS stamp also still carries it and is left alone deliberately — stamps are append-only history, corrected forward, not rewritten. **GATE-2 CROSS-CHECKS, ALL RUN, ALL RECORDED.** Rule 3b(vi-b) freshness sweep from the doc's OLDEST declared base (`deeb804`): **0** non-doc files changed, with a firing control (the unfiltered diff returns **5**, all under `design_docs/`) — so every V-row was measured against code identical to HEAD, which is exactly why V16 being wrong is a *designer* error and not a staleness artifact. ## STATUS 2026-08-06 (iteration 56) — **ITEM 10 `w-boundary-gate-tree-mutation` PROMOTED AHEAD OF `SM.B2a` AND ITS DESIGN DOC LANDED (PR #46 → squash `ca25ed6`; dev CI green BOTH jobs, SHA-addressed on the merge commit) — THE PROMOTION WAS EARNED BY A MEASUREMENT, NOT INHERITED FROM THE QUEUE ORDER.** **THE SPINE: A GREEN RESULT MUST BE UNABLE TO MEAN "THE CHECK NEVER RAN" — AND THIS ITERATION FOUND THAT SHAPE THREE TIMES, IN THE GATE, IN ITS OWN FIX, AND IN ITS OWN AUDIT.** Iteration 55 wrote *"consider ordering BEFORE `SM.B2a`"* and left the call open. Discharging it produced a hazard iter-55's row does **not** record: `mutateAndRestore` (`:190`) restores via **`defer`**, and a `defer` survives return and `t.Fatal` **and nothing else**. Measured first-party in worktrees of `origin/dev` @ `deeb804`, never the main tree: **SIGKILL** mid-mutation → `rc=137`, ` M host/store/store.go` **left on disk permanently**; **Go's OWN `-test.timeout` panic** → `250ms` `rc=2`, residue on `cmd/ailang-worldd/main.go`, against a firing **60s green control** that completes clean. **BOTH KILL PATHS LIVE INSIDE THE REPO'S OWN GATE**: `scripts/verify_go.sh` runs `go test ./... -race -timeout 8m` (the panic path) wrapped in an `os.killpg(…, SIGKILL)` at 600s (`:113`, the kill path), and CI job 2 invokes it. **THE RESIDUE IS INVISIBLE TO THE BUILD, WHICH IS WORSE THAN A BUILD BREAK**: with all three mutants applied simultaneously, `go build ./...` **rc=0** and `go vet` over all three groups **rc=0** — what it breaks is **the boundary gate itself**, which thereafter accuses an innocent production file of a *network-boundary violation* in wording indistinguishable from a real one, during the one milestone (`SM.B2a`) whose job is to add network code. That is the ordering argument, and it is stronger than the concurrency-window argument iter-55 recorded. **A HYPOTHESIS OF MINE WAS REFUTED EN ROUTE AND THE REFUTATION IS THE FINDING**: I predicted the residue would leave a NON-COMPILING file and brick the build. It does not — the gate's own inline comment already says *"compiling HTTP import"*. So the charter queue row, the iter-55 stamp and the dashboard were all wrong, three times over, to call the mutants *"deliberately non-compiling"*, and iter-55's transient `could not import net/http/httputil` CI red therefore **cannot** be explained by "the file is broken"; it is a build-graph artifact of the file *changing* under a concurrent build. Corrected in all three places this Gate 4. **DECISION**: `go list -overlay` + an overlay-aware read, so the gate never writes a byte under the repo root — closing crash-residue and the concurrency window with ONE mechanism. **Controller-verified rather than inherited**: the overlay closure is **diff-IDENTICAL** to a physically poisoned tree (control fired — baseline vs poisoned differ by **69** packages) and the repo tree stayed **0-dirty** throughout; the queue row's preferred scratch-copy repair was rejected because `repoRoot` bakes the real root in at **compile** time (`runtime.Caller(0)`) — a constraint I proved accidentally when a stale test binary from a deleted worktree made 8 timeout runs fail before reaching any mutation, i.e. eight clean-looking "no residue" results from an instrument that never ran. **TWO QUORUM ROUNDS, BOTH BLOCKED, BOTH ACCEPTED RATHER THAN ARGUED.** Round 1 — both reviewers, ONE objection: AC1/M3's ~2 ms polling detector was probabilistic, so a write-and-restore between ticks passed silently. They were right, and the irony is the spine: a detector whose green means *zero observations* is this item's own defect class. **Deleted, not supplemented**, and replaced by BOTH fixes as complementary layers — `gpt5-6-sol`'s structural write confinement (a writer rejecting any `repoRoot` destination) + a real `go/parser`+`go/ast` guard as the filesystem-independent PRIMARY, and `gemini-3-1-pro`'s `ModTime` check as a deterministic backstop. **Per rule 3f the controller MEASURED gemini's premise instead of forwarding it**: back-to-back write+restore advanced `st_mtime_ns` in **200/200** trials and **200/200** with a 1 ms gap, against a firing KP pair (no-write → unchanged `True`; single-write → changed 200/200) — so the backstop is deterministic, and the ext4 caveat is stated rather than assumed (measured on APFS only). Round 2 — TWO NEW objections, resolved under the **narrow-refinement carve-out** (precedent: iter-51 round 3); **neither disputed the design DIRECTION**, both carried a concrete reviewer-authored `proposed_fix`, so the applied text is the reviewers' **verbatim** remedy: (a) `gpt5-6-sol` — `AC4/M5`'s *"≥10 randomized offsets"* never established that any kill landed **after** an arm armed its overlay, so a clean tree could mean the threat was never exercised; replaced by its bounded barrier protocol (arm-specific ready marker, artifact verification, then SIGKILL, with a missing marker/timeout/absent artifact/early exit **failing** the criterion, one run per arm). (b) `gemini-3-1-pro` — **P9's audit had not searched for its own threat model**: `V15` grepped `os.WriteFile` alone while the design's Decision 8 names `os.OpenFile`/`os.Create`/`os.Rename` as mutators. **Gemini was right and the controller measured it two ways**: its widened search verbatim → **25** hits across 13 test files with `host/boundary` as the firing KP (**3**), the only `Create/OpenFile/Rename` outside `host/boundary` being `cli_test.go:272`, `t.TempDir()`-rooted; and then the property itself, measured end-to-end rather than read off 25 call sites — sampling `git status --porcelain` at 20 Hz across the WHOLE suite gives **1** distinct dirty observation with the gate enabled (` M cmd/ailang-worldd/main.go` — the sampler fires) and **0** with only `TestWorldBoundaryDependencyAllowlist` skipped. **P9 is now a measurement, not a search.** Both arms also exited `rc=1` on the SAME pre-existing cause and it was chased rather than waved through: `TestEpisodeLiveReplayThreeArmsAndEvidence` → *"AILANG_BIN must name the pinned released interpreter"*, which is `verify_go.sh`'s deliberate fail-loud guard firing at a bare `go test` that bypassed the script — identical in both arms, so the comparison is unaffected, and CI is green at `deeb804`. **A SECOND, PRE-EXISTING FINDING, CONTROLLER-CONFIRMED AND CARRIED AS `10/OD-1` RATHER THAN FIXED**: `checkGoGroup` (`:130`) computes the `go list -deps` closure and then uses it **only** as a `len(deps)==0` anti-vacuity guard — the forbidden-prefix scan walks **direct import specs alone**, so a TRANSITIVE forbidden dependency is invisible in a gate named *"dependency allowlist"*. **INSTRUMENT DISCIPLINE, INCLUDING MY OWN FAILURES:** my first SIGKILL sampler recomputed baselines via `git show` per round and lost the race to the restore — that result was **UNINFORMATIVE, not negative**, and was re-run with precomputed baselines before anything was believed; ## STATUS 2026-08-06 (iteration 55) — **`AC12` REPAIRED (PR #45 → squash `1761a9c`; dev CI green BOTH jobs, SHA-addressed on the merge commit) — THE CARRY-FORWARD THE LAST TWO ITERATIONS WROTE DOWN WAS DISCHARGED EARLY, AND DISCHARGING IT EARLY IS WHAT FOUND THE HOLE.** **THE SPINE: AN EXCEPTION IS A CLAIM ABOUT ONE THING, AND THE CHEAPEST WAY TO LOSE A GUARD IS TO EXPRESS THAT EXCEPTION AS AN OMISSION FROM A SHARED LIST.** Iterations 53 and 54 both recorded, in as many words, that `AC12`'s *"network confined to `host/broker`"* control is **VACUOUS until SM.B2a** and must be re-asserted there rather than inherited as green. This iteration re-asserted it **at the boundary, before the network code exists** — and the gate turned out to be weaker than vacuous in a second way nobody had recorded. `host/boundary/allowlist_world_test.go` protects three Go groups, and its loopback exception is true of **exactly one** of them: `cmd/ailang-worldd` genuinely needs `net/http` for its loopback-only daemon client, and its own code comment at `:31-33` says so. But the exception was expressed by **omitting bare `net/http` from the single global `forbiddenImportPrefixes`**, so `host/store` and `host/replay` silently inherited it. **Measured first-party, every mutation confirmed landed by sha256 BEFORE the result was believed, every restore verified byte-identical**: bare `net/http` blank-imported into `host/store/store.go` (`40315426…`→`53b45ebd…`) left the gate **rc=0 PASS**; into `host/replay/replay.go` likewise **rc=0 PASS**; while the `net/http/httputil` arm correctly **rc=1 RED**, naming the exact path — so the instrument worked and the threat still walked through it. **THE REASON IT SURVIVED IS ITERATION 54'S OWN SPINE, ARRIVING INSIDE THE GATE ITERATION 53 LANDED**: every protected group's `mutantImport` was `net/http/httputil`, so the gate had only ever been tested against **a mutation shaped to the check rather than to the threat**. **AND THE EXEMPTION WAS UNFORCED, NOT MERELY UNNECESSARY — the alternative explanation was measured and REFUTED (rule 3d) rather than assumed**: the natural defence is *"bare `net/http` must already be in those closures, so forbidding it would red the baseline."* Baseline `net/http` presence per dependency closure is `host/store` **0** (160 deps), `host/replay` **0** (162), `cmd/ailang-worldd` **1** (233), control `host/hashref` **0**. Only one group ever needed the exception; the other two could always have been held to the stricter rule. Repaired with a per-group `extraForbidden` field — the exception now lives where it is true — plus `TestBareNetHTTPExemptionIsPerGroup`, which pins the asymmetry so collapsing it back into one global list **fails loudly** instead of silently re-granting the exemption; proven non-vacuous by setting `host/store`'s entry to `nil` → **RED naming the group** (`forbiddenImport("net/http") for host/store = false, want true`), restore byte-identical. Post-repair, all five arms: both threats **RED**, `httputil` still **RED**, `cmd/ailang-worldd` still **PASS**, pristine **green**. **WHAT SM.B2a STILL MUST RE-ASSERT, AND MUST NOT INHERIT:** the *positive* half is still unproven — that network code, once it EXISTS in `host/broker`, is genuinely permitted there. `host/broker` has **zero** `net/http` deps at HEAD (control: 8 files repo-wide carry it), and the gate's green control asserts only that the broker's dependency closure is **NON-EMPTY** (`:281`), which is true of every Go package alive. **THE SECOND FINDING IS PRE-EXISTING, WAS NOT IN THE PICK, AND IS NOW QUEUE ITEM 10.** The same gate proves its teeth by **rewriting three OTHER packages' production source files in the live working tree** (`mutateAndRestore` at `:271` → `cmd/ailang-worldd/main.go`, `host/store/store.go`, `host/replay/replay.go`, each with a deliberately non-compiling import) while `go test ./...` builds those packages **concurrently**. It fired here: `go test ./...` red-lit `TestCLIRealSubprocessEpisode` with `cmd/ailang-worldd/main.go:27:4: could not import net/http/httputil` — `cli_test.go:128` builds the subprocess binary from source and sampled the file inside the gate's own window. **Attribution is by MECHANISM, not co-occurrence** (rule 3d): `httputil` is precisely this gate's `mutantImport` for that group and nothing else in the repo inserts that string into that file. **Then measured directly on a PRISTINE tree with this iteration's change ABSENT** (`origin/dev` @ `ff5d5cc`, `0` modified): sampling during ONE gate run observed `main.go` mutated **5** times, `store.go` **5**, `replay.go` **5**, out of **90** samples, against a **0 / 200** control with the gate idle — so the broken state is genuinely published to concurrent readers and the sampler is not always-true. **Rate honestly bounded and the instrument honestly labelled**: the first attempt to reproduce used a *narrowed* command (two packages, not `./...`) and returned 0/12 — that measured the instrument, not the phenomenon (Gate 1's own lesson), so it was re-run identically to CI's form: **0 / 8** runs of `go test ./... -count=1` on pristine `dev`. Latent, not active; queued rather than fix-forwarded because the principled repair (mutate a scratch COPY, not the live tree) changes what the gate proves and wants a designer + evaluator. It is recorded now because **SM.B2a lengthens the `host/broker` suite** (already 76 s under `-race`), which widens the window rather than narrowing it. **PICK DISCIPLINE.** Item 8 was the pick and `AC12` is item 8's own criterion, so this is the item's prerequisite slice, not a second item; **`SM.B2a` was deliberately NOT started** — a ~780 LOC milestone that introduces the repo's first irreversible-publish-capable code deserves a full executor+evaluator iteration against the repaired gate, not a rushed tail-end run, and iter-52's plan explicitly budgets buffer iterations. **GATE-2 CROSS-CHECKS, ALL RUN, ALL RECORDED.** Rule 3b(vii) doc↔plan diff (the doc's mtime is LATER than the plan's — positive evidence of divergence): the DOC assigns SM.B = {`AC7`,`AC8`,`AC9`,`AC10`,`AC11`,`AC12`}; the PLAN splits SM.B2a = {`AC7`,`AC10`,`AC11`}, SM.B2b = {`AC8`,`AC9`,`AC9a`,`AC9b`,`AC9c`}, with `AC12` moved to SM.A — **the partition is COMPLETE, no AC dropped**, and the doc predates the B2a/B2b split so the PLAN wins on assignment. ## STATUS 2026-08-05 (iteration 54) — **`w-self-mod-vertical` MILESTONE `SM.B1` LANDED (PR #43 → squash `1856bfb`; dev CI green BOTH jobs, SHA-addressed on the merge commit) — AND BOTH OF THIS ITERATION'S FINDINGS ARE THE SAME DEFECT WEARING DIFFERENT CLOTHES: A CHECK THAT WAS NEVER POINTED AT THE THING IT GUARDS.** **THE SPINE: A MUTATION SHAPED TO THE CHECK TESTS THE CHECK, NOT THE THREAT.** `SM.B1`'s `AC-B1.2` is the milestone's own declared gating criterion — `schemaV2SQL` must be AUTHORED, never derived from `schemaSQL`, or the ledger compares the schema to itself and the gate is a no-op. `TestSchemaVersionLedgerIsIndependent` greps **its own source file**; its two **negative** needles were carefully split (`"var schemaV2SQL = "+"schemaSQL"`) so they would not match the very lines containing them, while its **positive** needle was one literal, `"const schemaV2SQL = schemaV1SQL +"`, which **the check's own line satisfies** — so it passed no matter what the declaration said. The asymmetry IS the lesson: the split was applied exactly where omitting it makes a check fire **ALWAYS** (immediate, unmissable symptom) and omitted where it makes a check fire **NEVER** (no symptom at all). Mechanism: `"$BIN" --version | grep -q 'AILANG v0.30.0'` under `set -o pipefail` — `grep -q` exits at its FIRST match while `--version` still has **6 of its 7 lines** to write, the writer takes SIGPIPE, and `pipefail` promotes that to the step's status. Whether it fires is a scheduling race, which is exactly why the identical step passed on `e24a6f0`, `1856bfb` and both PR runs. **Measured, not inferred**: piped form **3/40** runs non-zero on the real binary; no-pipe form **0/200**; deterministic repro `{ echo match; seq 1 200000; } | grep -q match` under `pipefail` → **141** every time. **THE FIRST FIX WAS WRONG AND THE STRESS ARM CAUGHT IT** — capturing to a variable is NOT sufficient, because `echo "$big" | grep -q` reproduces 141 too (the builtin writer blocks on the same pipe); the fix is **no pipe at all** (`case`). **Both** call sites converted, not just the red one: the second (`AILANG_BIN`) has never failed but **only by ACCIDENT OF SIZE** — `--version` is **167 bytes**, which fits the 64 KiB pipe buffer, so `echo` never blocks. That is a property of the output LENGTH, not of the shape; same race, one verbose release away. Non-vacuity preserved (a `v0.33.0` string still exits 1; `case` treats `.` literally, so it is marginally STRICTER than the `grep` it replaces). Validated `actionlint` rc=0, all **6** `run:` blocks `bash -n` rc=0, no code-level `| grep -q` remaining against a firing control. PR #44 → squash **`bbee51c`**; **dev CI GREEN both jobs, SHA-addressed**. So this iteration landed **four** commits and its three findings rhyme: a ledger check that never looked at the threat, a gate suite that never looked at file types, and a version assertion whose *plumbing* could fail independently of what it asserted.

The bounded re-run, using a *better* discriminator (World stores leave `<db>.writer.lock` and `<db>.artifacts/` sidecars, which are independent of the `.db` extension) with the control firing, found **ZERO World stores on the rig**: 13 candidate SQLite files inspected **read-only through the pinned `modernc.org/sqlite v1.54.0` driver** (never the C CLI) all carry **0/7** canonical tables, `/tmp/world-demo.db` from QUICKSTART is tmp-reaped, and the single World-shaped hit is an **orphaned `world.db.artifacts` directory in a `mktemp` dir whose database no longer exists**. Coordinator attestation **VERIFIED**; no unexpected store; **no re-park**. The control store the controller created for the sweep was itself deleted afterwards — it was a version-0 World store, and leaving it would have been the very thing being swept for. **A FALSE RED THE CONTROLLER ALMOST OWNED**: the first full `go test ./...` outside the sandbox failed `host/broker` with *"AILANG_BIN must name the pinned released interpreter"* — reproduced on a **clean `origin/dev`** worktree without the pin and **green with it**, so it is M6's anti-false-green guard firing on a misconfigured instrument, not a DG.B regression. **`zsh` COST A REWORK AGAIN (class instance 5)**: `titles=( "" a b c d )` is **1-indexed** in zsh, so the first reconstruction pass shifted every commit message by one while the *content* stayed correct; caught by reading the log, fixed by discarding the four commits and rebuilding with an explicit `case` — the tree was proven identical before discarding. **WEEKLY EXTERNAL-ISSUE SWEEP (new Gate-0 duty): CLEAN** — one open issue in this repo (`#32`, the bookkeeping thread itself), zero-mention count **0**, nothing to triage. **Queue: 4d COMPLETE — both milestones LANDED, doc + its sprint-plan companion → `design_docs/implemented/`; `4e` RG.A and `4f` branch-A remain routable and untouched** (Standing rule 1). `4d/OD-4` (fail vs migrate) stays a recorded FUTURE decision, not a blocker: its own recommendation is alternative 1, fail-loud-only, which is exactly what DG.B implements. ## STATUS 2026-08-03 (iteration 44) — **`w-ddl-gate-teeth` DG.B DESIGNED — THE VERSION PIN, AND THE FRESHNESS TEST THAT CALLED A POPULATED STORE EMPTY** (PR #34 → squash `6b8e77e`, dev CI green **both jobs SHA-addressed on the merge commit**; quorum **2 rounds, BOTH BLOCKED, narrow-refinement carve-out APPLIED as the bounded 2nd revision**; designer `codex:gpt-5.6-sol` (rotation) fired **twice** — original + the one sanctioned revision — planner/executor/evaluator **not fired**, a design iteration; **`metered=$0.3192`** of the `$5` ceiling; controller `claude-opus-5`; rotation pointer **ADVANCED to `codex:gpt-5.6-sol`**). **THE ITERATION'S SPINE: A KNOWN-POSITIVE CONTROL PROVES AN INSTRUMENT *CAN* FIRE — IT DOES NOT PROVE IT FIRES *ONLY WHERE IT SHOULD*, AND THE UNTESTED HALF IS THE EXCLUSION BOUNDARY.** DG.B is the half of Mark's attended ratification that was **ratified but never designed**: `4d/OD-3` was a three-alternative decision packet with no ACs, no named mutations and no fixtures, so this iteration produced the **design**, not the code — item 4d stays open on implementation. **THE CENTRAL PROBLEM, MEASURED RATHER THAN ASSUMED**: on the day the pin lands a brand-new store and a decade-old legacy store **both** read `user_version=0` (V-C/V-M), and production performs **zero** `sqlite_master` inspection (V-E, control firing in `journal_test.go`), so nothing can tell them apart — making "reject 0" brick every existing store and "accept 0" the inert-marker shape OD-3's own text warns against. D5 resolves it by classifying **before** `schemaSQL` runs; D6 gives legacy/future/invalid **distinct typed errors**; D7 commits schema + pin in **one transaction**; D8 extends the boundary to `OpenReadOnly`; D9 freezes an **independent** version-1 ledger. **EVERY LOAD-BEARING PREMISE WAS RE-MEASURED THROUGH THE PINNED `modernc.org/sqlite v1.54.0` DRIVER RATHER THAN THE `sqlite3` C CLI** — the instrument gap iteration 43's planner recorded as **F4**, closed here by construction: rows **V-I…V-S** are controller first-party. The most load-bearing was **V-I**: `PRAGMA user_version` **IS** transactional (in-tx `1`, after `Rollback()` **`0` with 0 application objects**, control set-`5`-outside-tx reads `5`), so D7's induced-failure criterion **AC7 is achievable at all** — had the pragma escaped the transaction, the milestone would have shipped a vacuous atomicity gate. **THREE DEFECTS, AND THE QUORUM EARNED ITS COST ON EVERY ONE.** (1) **`gemini-3-1-pro` and the controller INDEPENDENTLY AND SEPARATELY** found that DG.B makes DG.A's **AC2 vacuous** — the criterion iteration 43 called *"the required discriminating M4/M5 control"*. `MUT-EXISTING-DDL-CHANGE-REMANIFESTED` alters neither `user_version` nor the application-object count, and D5 classifies on exactly those two, so after DG.B the pre-journal test returns the same typed legacy error **with the mutation applied and unapplied** — it passes both ways. gemini: *"rendering AC2 mathematically impossible to satisfy as written."* Two parties reaching one conclusion by different routes is why this is recorded as the round's finding rather than either party's. Fixed **without deletion**, per D4's own convention: AC2's original text and mutation are **retained verbatim and marked SUPERSEDED**, the vacuity stated in plain words, the mutation **re-pointed to AC10**, the "existing store never upgraded" property **reassigned to AC6** as a PRODUCTION discriminator with the argument that door-rejection is *stronger* than post-open DDL comparison because no typed `Store` escapes, and AC2 given its own new production RED `MUT-LEGACY-REJECTION-BYPASS`. (2) **`gpt5-6-sol`: D5's case table (0 / 1 / >1) was NOT TOTAL** — a negative `user_version` matched no branch. **Measured rather than forwarded, and it came back WORSE THAN FILED**: negatives round-trip and **persist across close/reopen** (`-1`, INT32_MIN), **and the field truncates SILENTLY** — `2147483648` and `4294967296` both succeed and read back **`0`** (V-N…V-R). So a store stamped by a future binary above INT32_MAX classifies as *version 0*, and if structurally empty would be **initialized and stamped version 1** — the detector erasing its own evidence. That truncation is a **controller finding, not the reviewer's**. Fixed: the table is now total and mutually exclusive over the signed-32-bit domain (invalid / fresh / legacy / supported / future), a new `InvalidSchemaVersionError`, AC12 + AC13, and a Design Freeze bullet pinning every schema version to `1 .. 2147483647` with the truncation measurement as the stated reason. (3) **`gpt5-6-sol` round 2: THE FRESHNESS PREDICATE ITSELF WAS UNSOUND, AND ITS `catch` LANDED ON MY OWN VERIFICATION ROW.** In SQL `LIKE`, `_` is a **single-character wildcard**, so `name NOT LIKE 'sqlite_%'` excludes every name beginning `sqlite` **plus any character** — not the literal reserved prefix. **V-S, measured before adopting the fix**: SQLite **rejects** `sqlite_internal_probe` (*"object name reserved for internal use"*) but **ACCEPTS** `sqliteX_probe`, and a version-0 store whose only application object is `sqliteX_probe` counts **0** under the shipped predicate — classified **FRESH**, then initialized and **version-stamped over real application data** — against **1** under `NOT LIKE 'sqlite\_%' ESCAPE '\'`, with a two-sided control confirming genuine `sqlite_` internals are still excluded and `sqliteX_probe` no longer is. **The sharp part is whose instrument failed**: my own **V-K** had *just* re-checked this predicate against the iteration-41 `sqlite_%` scar and pronounced it LIVE — because I proved the limb **fires** (a known-positive matched) and **never tested the other direction**, whether a name that should SURVIVE the filter does. A known-positive control establishes an instrument *can* match; it says nothing about what it *wrongly* matches. **→ Gate-5 process fix this iteration.** **CARVE-OUT, APPLIED DELIBERATELY AND RECORDED**: round 2 was `gemini-3-1-pro` **PASS** + controller **PASS** + one reject whose `proposed_fix` was concrete, reviewer-authored, and disputed **no part of the direction** — a one-predicate soundness bug plus fixtures — so the narrow-refinement carve-out applied (as at iters 13 and 40) and the reviewer's fix was taken **verbatim** as AC14 + `MUT-PREFIX-WILDCARD-REGRESSION`, not paraphrased. **THE HONEST TALLY WAS CORRECTED TWICE ACROSS THE CYCLE — 6/11 → 9/13 → 10/14 production discriminators** — because Objection 1 was itself a tally-honesty failure, and the doc now carries **18** premise rows and **9** primary named mutations. **CF-O-1, FOUND WHILE APPLYING THE FIX AND RECORDED RATHER THAN SILENTLY WIDENED INTO SCOPE**: the same unescaped predicate sits in **LANDED DG.A code** at `journal_test.go:877`, so AC1's *"unexpected tables fail loudly"* cannot see a `sqliteX_probe` table — impact low (hardcoded seven-table manifest, one site, control-verified), but it means iteration 41's V-row calling that filter a harmless *"dormant guard"* was **understated**. Left for DG.B's executor, who edits adjacent code. **PARKED FOR MARK — ONE NEW ASK, `4d/OD-7`**: DG.B may be implemented and mutation-tested, but **rollout onto a rig holding valuable version-0 stores is blocked** — the repository cannot establish whether the rig's existing stores are disposable, reconstructible or authoritative, and blindly stamping `user_version=1` would prove only that a pragma was writable and would recreate the inert-marker defect, so the design contains **no command or helper that does it**. DG.A replaces it with a **hardcoded seven-table canonical manifest** plus `preJournalSchemaV0`, a **verbatim fixture from `8133573`** (sha256 `35f09862e2…`, structurally independent of the embedded `schemaSQL` — `//go:embed` vs a compile-time `const`, so no runtime path can derive one from the other). **THE LOAD-BEARING MEASUREMENT, REPRODUCED FIRST-PARTY BY THE CONTROLLER AND THEN AGAIN BY THE EVALUATOR RATHER THAN INHERITED FROM THE EXECUTOR**: apply a real `store_heads` DDL edit **and legitimately re-manifest it** — the same one-line update the old pin invited — and the fresh-store gate goes **GREEN** while `TestOpenAddsJournalAndDetectsStalePreJournalDDL` **REDS** at `journal_test.go:870` naming `store_heads`, printing got (2 cols) vs want (3 cols). That asymmetry *is* the de-fang Mark ratified, and it is why the fix is not "a better hash": no single edit re-greens both halves, because the historical fixture is not derived from the thing being edited. **RATIFICATION SCOPE — THE HALF THAT IS NOT DESIGNED, AND WHY IT WAS NOT BUILT.** Mark's stamp ratified **two** things: the sha256 de-fang **and** a `PRAGMA user_version` pin failing LOUD on binary↔store mismatch, with the frozen-store touch ratified. The de-fang is exactly what DG.A already specified (AC4 deletes the pin; AC2 is the discriminating control), so it shipped. The `user_version` contract is **RATIFIED BUT UNDESIGNED** — OD-3 is a three-alternative decision packet with **no ACs, no named mutations, no fixtures**, and its own text demands legacy-version-0 treatment be specified and the fresh/supported/legacy/future cases be proven before ratification-class code lands. **Ratifying a DECISION is not the same as having a DESIGN**, and building a durability-kernel acceptance contract headlessly from a decision packet is the fabrication Gate 2 forbids — so it is carried as **DG.B**, queued for a designer, and **item 4d is NOT closed**. The doc's D4 position was **retained verbatim as the argument that lost** rather than quietly rewritten to look prescient, and `gpt5-6-sol` — who rejected twice on this exact direction — is recorded as having been right. **A LIVE OD-NUMBER COLLISION THAT INVERTS A RATIFICATION, FOUND BY READING THE STAMP CAREFULLY RATHER THAN QUICKLY.** Mark's single sentence contains *"4d RATIFIED — fix NOW: … `user_version` pin"* **and** *"OD-3 stays declined-as-primary"*. Both are true and they are about **different items**: `OD-3` means the `user_version` contract in 4d's doc and *"change the two `scan.go` sites"* in 4e's, and `OD-4` likewise means two different things. Read carelessly, the same stamp un-ratifies the fix it orders. 4d's own numbering note was written **against precisely this** (citing the iter-31 ID-collision defect) but only checked the **charter's parked list**, not the sibling **doc** — so the remedy missed instance three, in the very document it was written into. Verified first-party by enumerating `### OD-` headings across all three head docs with a known-positive control. **→ Gate-5 process fix this iteration: OD IDs are now a mission-global namespace with a registry.** **HONEST SCOPE, STATED BECAUSE THE NUMBER IS TEMPTING**: **two** ACs are production discriminators (AC1, AC2 — each reddened by a PRODUCTION mutation), **three** are test-side probes (AC3–AC5) proving gate independence, not kernel properties; *"5/5 acceptance criteria with named REDs"* would overstate the production evidence by **2.5×**. AC3/AC5's mutations remain **executor-run and not re-verified first-party** — labelled, not papered over. **`store.Open` STILL ACCEPTS A STRUCTURALLY STALE STORE AND RETURNS `nil`** (iter-41's M5, untouched): DG.A makes drift visible **in the suite before a change lands** and makes nothing visible to an already-deployed binary. **INSTRUMENT DISCIPLINE.** The executor's own gate verdict was treated as **UNINFORMATIVE** and every gate re-run outside its `workspace-write` sandbox (a loopback-bind denial is indistinguishable from a regression in an exit code, and it hides real failures as readily as it invents them). But R4, the rule that decides a pair is a valid cost claim, constrains only `control.commit == variant.parent` plus identical `goversion`, `goos_goarch`, `ncpu`, `hw_model`. **A search of the whole document for any temporal, load-comparability, pair-identity or control-reuse constraint returns NOTHING** (only the words "reuse"/"collision" in the Conflict Surface's own column headers), while the **known-positive control in the same call** confirms the schema *does* carry `utc`, `load_before`, `load_after`. So an idle variant pairs with a control recorded days earlier under heavy load and **passes** — reintroducing the **6.06×** artefact class inside the artifact built to eliminate it. **SEVENTH INSTANCE of this mission's signature shape, and its THIRD inside this one document's evolution**: round 1 carried it as the caller-cwd toolchain probe (a gate comparing a value to itself), the revision fixed that and **kept the bug as a named mutation** (`MUT-PROBE-CALLER-DIR`, which must *green* a known-cross-toolchain pair), and round 2 surfaced the same shape one level up in the pairing rule itself. **BOTH ROUND-1 OBJECTIONS WERE RIGHT AND BOTH ADOPTED.** `gpt5-6-sol`: the recorder ran `go test -bench` with no wall-clock ceiling (Standing Rule 6), and `go test -timeout` is **insufficient** — it bounds the test binary's clock, not the compile step or a wedged child; fixed by **mirroring `run_bounded` from `scripts/verify_ail.sh:61-74`** (read first-party: python3 `start_new_session`, `os.killpg` SIGKILL, exit 124, hardcoded deadlines), timeout = named refusal emitting **zero fences**, `MUT-REC-STALL` proving the kill reaches a **grandchild**. `gemini-3-1-pro`: `go env GOVERSION` was probed in the caller's directory — **and I MEASURED THE PREMISE RATHER THAN FORWARDING IT**, which made it sharper than filed: with `GOTOOLCHAIN=auto` live, a module with floor `go 1.26.5` reads **`go1.26.5`**, this repo (floor 1.26.4) reads **`go1.26.4`**, no module reads `go1.26.4` — so `GOVERSION` is **directory-sensitive**, and because **OD-1 proposes lowering this repo's floor**, the first A/B pair straddling it is *exactly* the cross-toolchain pair R4 exists to red, which the round-1 design would have greened by comparing the variant's toolchain to itself. Fix verified with a control in the same call (`go -C <module floor 1.26.5> env GOVERSION` → `go1.26.5`). **THE DESIGNER SELF-CAUGHT THE ITERATION-40 TRAP**: its first draft of the bounding fix bounded only the `go` invocations and left `$AILANG_BIN --version` **unbounded inside the remedy for unbounded waits** — found in its own re-read, now bounded in D1, the deadline bullet and the Design Freeze. ## STATUS 2026-07-30 (iteration 41) — **`w-ddl-gate-teeth` (item 4d): DOC + FIVE MEASUREMENTS LANDED; THE REPAIR ITSELF IS PARKED ON A COLLISION BETWEEN TWO RATIFIED GUARDRAILS** (PR #30 → squash `d56da6f`, dev CI green **both jobs SHA-addressed on the merge commit and the step logs read to prove they ran**; quorum **2 rounds, both BLOCKED, carve-out deliberately NOT applied**; `metered=$0.1155`; designer `codex:gpt-5.6-sol` fired twice — original + the one sanctioned revision — planner/executor/evaluator **not fired**; controller `claude-opus-5`). **THE ITERATION'S SPINE: A GATE WHOSE SANCTIONED REPAIR RE-GREENS IT IS NOT A GATE — THE DOCUMENTED FIX IS THE VULNERABILITY.** Five iterations of this mission have hunted *gates that cannot fail*; item 4d's gate is a **different and worse species**. `TestPreJournalMigrationPreservesExistingDDL` pins the sha256 of `schema.sql`'s pre-journal prefix, so a real DDL edit **does** red — with a message handing the developer the new hash. Paste it back, the single action the message invites, and **all 10 packages go green while the edit remains unapplied to every existing store**. That is **M4 `MUT-DDL-DRIFT-REPINNED`**, new this iteration; the queue row carried M1–M3 and stopped one step short of the thing that makes them matter. **A change detector that costs one line to silence is not a correctness gate, and its green is worse than no gate because the green gets cited.** **FIVE MEASUREMENTS, FIRST-PARTY AT `e5027df`, EACH REVERTED BY `cp` WITH sha256 PRINTED BOTH SIDES; THE THREE INHERITED CLAIMS ALL HOLD** but were re-measured rather than restated, the row's evidence being from `0f2afad` with three milestones landed since. **M1** widening journal's `kind` CHECK leaves `go test ./...` **rc=0, 10/10 green** — and the green was **proven to be blindness, not a no-op mutation**, by a `sqlite3` probe reading the materialized DDL **containing `MUTANT`** with the unmutated backup as the control in the same call (`store.go:262` execs `schemaSQL` verbatim, so it is what production ships). **M2** a real `store_heads` column reds **exactly one test**, at `journal_test.go:636` — the source pin, which `t.Fatalf`s **before line 638 opens a database**, so the `sqlite_master` comparison the doc-set credits **never executes**. **M3** short-circuiting that comparison (both vars used, `go vet` rc=0) leaves package and full suite **green** — zero discrimination. **M5**, the one that shows the stakes: an old store opened by a binary built from the new schema, through **real `store.Open`**, returns **`err=nil`** with the DDL **byte-identical before and after** — *not applied* is indistinguishable from *applied*. The comparison is **self-referential by construction** (`before` comes from the same already-edited source `Open` will exec) so it can only ever fire for a newly added non-journal table, and `journal` is excluded by an explicit `delete(after, "journal")`. **WHY IT PARKS, AND WHY THAT IS THE CHARTER WORKING RATHER THAN A STALL.** `gpt5-6-sol` rejected in both rounds on a **direction** dispute — sharpened the second time to *"axiom noncompliance, not merely an out-of-scope enhancement"* — so the narrow-refinement carve-out, which requires every remaining objection to be non-directional, **does not apply and was not stretched**. What is actually in front of the loop is a collision between **two ratified guardrails**: the **no-silent-fallback axiom** (shipping a test-only detector closes the item while production still returns success for a structurally stale store) and **frozen-kernel discipline** (changing which on-disk stores `store.Open` accepts requires explicit human ratification). Standing rule 2 forbids forcing past the objection; the charter forbids adopting the reviewer's fix headlessly. **A headless loop must not pick a winner between two rules Mark ratified** — so it became **OD-5**, with both sides at full strength and a recommendation, and the reviewer is **not wrong on the facts** (I measured the fail-open; until OD-3 ships, deployed binaries cannot diagnose drift) — only about who may decide. **THE QUORUM'S LEDGER IS THE ARGUMENT FOR RUNNING IT: FOUR OBJECTIONS, THREE RIGHT, ONE WRONG IN ITS PRESCRIPTION.** Right: fixture provenance, durable-table enumeration, determinism (Go map iteration is randomised), and — **r2, load-bearing, and neither the designer nor I had checked it** — whether the historical artifact's DDL still matches HEAD, because if it did not the proposed test would **RED ON BASELINE** and the design would be broken as written (measured: **all 6 tables identical, zero drift**, premise HOLDS). Wrong: `gemini-3-1-pro` asked to log **`name NOT LIKE 'sqlite_%'`** as `tableDDL`'s filter, but on a materialized store `sqlite_master` holds **14** rows — 7 tables plus **7 `sqlite_autoindex_*` of `type='index'`** — so `count(type='table' AND name NOT LIKE 'sqlite_%')` and `count(type='table')` are **both 7**: that clause excludes **nothing today** and the live limb is `type='table'`. Adopting it on the reviewer's authority would have **logged a dead mechanism as the live one — iteration 38's exact defect, reproduced inside the remedy for it.** Separately, `gpt5-6-sol`'s provenance ask **improved on what both the doc and the reviewer proposed**: `d5774eb` added the journal table, so `8133573:host/store/schema.sql` is the last pre-journal schema (sha256 `35f09862e2…`, 6 tables) and the fixture is now sourced **verbatim from a pinned real artifact** instead of hand-authored. **AN HONESTY GAP NEITHER REVIEWER NAMED**: the designer's D2 test was `TestOpenUpgradesPreJournalStoreToCurrentDDL` and would **pass** while `store.Open` performs no upgrade at all beyond creating the absent `journal` table — a green test whose *name* asserts the protection the design explicitly disclaims, in the artifact a future reader greps first (iter-38's class). Renamed **`TestOpenAddsJournalAndDetectsStalePreJournalDDL`**, honest claim required **beside the test**, not only in the doc's tail. **A NEW ZSH INSTRUMENT DEFECT — THIRD IN THIS CLASS, AND IT RETURNED A PLAUSIBLE NUMBER RATHER THAN AN ERROR.** `git show "$c:host/store/schema.sql"` reported **`total_tables=0` for the commit that created the schema**: zsh parses `$c:h` as the **`:h` dirname modifier**, so the path became `.ost/store/schema.sql`. Isolated with a **bash control** on identical strings (zsh 5.9: `:h`→`.ost/x`, `:t`, `:r`, `:e` all bite; `"$c:store/x"` is a hard `bad substitution`; bash leaves all five literal). Fix: `"${c}:host/…"`. It matters **here** because `git show "$rev:path"` is the canonical archaeology idiom, the shared skill's Gate 1 prescribes that very form, **every Go file in this repo lives under `host/`**, and with stderr redirected the failure becomes a plausible `grep -c` zero. Scoped honestly: **no committed script uses `git show` at all** (control-verified), so it is a loop-instrument defect, not a defect in landed code. **A MEASUREMENT COST OF THE PARKED OD-1 THAT NOBODY HAD PRICED**: every local `host/store` measurement now requires **editing `go.mod`**, because `GOTOOLCHAIN=go1.25.6` is refused outright (`go.mod requires go >= 1.26.4`) — the `go` directive is a **floor**, so the corrective toolchain is **unselectable** while OD-1 sits parked. Every number in this iteration is conditioned on a throwaway worktree with the floor lowered, stated rather than glossed; it is a recurring per-iteration tax and now a cost row on OD-1 (**CF-L-3**). **Four of my own instruments failed and every one was caught by a control or assertion I ran on purpose** — the `:h` defect (first symptom hidden by my own `2>/dev/null`), a `grep -cE "ok  +github"` returning **0** against a 69 KB CI log because `go test` prints `ok`+**TAB** (true reading: **10 packages ok**), an empty `--unread` control that was a wrong pattern rather than a broken tool, and a Gate-4 doc edit that **refused to apply** because its anchor's trailing double-space had been stripped — the `assert count==1` guard caught it instead of silently mis-editing. **Queue: 4d [PARKED needs-human-review on OD-5] — doc + evidence landed, DG.A specified but NOT routable; 4f `w-bench-load-confound` is now the only unblocked head item**, deliberately left again because its CF-K-2 would bank a toolchain condition that OD-1 is parked to invalidate. New carry-forwards **CF-L-1** (DG.A, blocked on OD-5), **CF-L-2** (`schema_ddl_test.go` split, cosmetic), **CF-L-3** (the `go.mod`-floor tax → OD-1). Rotation pointer **ADVANCED to `codex:gpt-5.6-sol`** (a designer that ran advances it). 4 stamps → newest 3 kept (iter-41, iter-40, iter-39); the positionally-4th (iter-38) archived this Gate 4.

## STATUS 2026-07-30 (iteration 40) — **`w-race-gate-blindspot` (item 4e): MECHANISM IDENTIFIED — IT IS A GO COMPILER REGRESSION, NOT A SQLITE BUG, AND IT DOES NOT NEED `-race`** (PR #29 → squash `c90713b`, dev CI green SHA-addressed on the merge commit; doc + re-runnable reproduction fixture LANDED; **remediation PARKED `needs-human-review` on OD-1/OD-2**; quorum **2 rounds, both BLOCKED, carve-out revision applied**, `metered=$0.123`; planner/executor/designer **not fired** — an investigation iteration; controller `claude-opus-5`). **THE ITERATION'S SPINE: THE COMPILER IS AN INSTRUMENT TOO, AND NOTHING IN THIS REPOSITORY WAS CHECKING IT.** Item 4e had sat for three iterations as *"a test that fails deterministically because the gate that would see it is never run"*, with the mechanism labelled UNKNOWN and a standing hypothesis of `modernc.org/sqlite`'s `unsafe` usage interacting with `-race`'s memory layout. **That hypothesis is REFUTED.** go **1.26.0 → 1.26.5** — 1.26.5 being the **latest stable**, so there is nothing to upgrade forward to — miscompile, on darwin/arm64, a **local array literal indexed by a `range` variable** assigned into a struct string field inside a composite literal that also carries an interface method call: the field comes back with a **corrupt string header** (nil data pointer, garbage length `4334851712`), so merely printing it SIGSEGVs. **A 52-line program with ZERO dependencies and NO `-race` reproduces it under a plain `go build`**; `-gcflags=all=-N` fixes it, `all=-l` does not (the optimizer, not inlining); go1.25.6 and go1.24.9 are clean. **ONE mechanism explains BOTH of iter-39's two symptoms** — the same one-package `-N` switch clears the *failure* of `TestScanUnreadableLogKeysetResumes` **and** the *hang* of `TestScanUnreadableWorldsFindsPoison` — which is exactly what iter-39 demanded of any candidate. Census: **exactly two** production sites carry the shape, `host/store/scan.go:74` and `:112`, which are precisely the two functions the two symptoms belong to; **`scan.go` is correct Go and needs no correctness fix.** And the `-race` leg the item was asking for is **viable and cheap**: with `GOTOOLCHAIN=go1.25.6` + `AILANG_BIN` set, `go test ./... -race` is **rc=0, 10/10 packages, 179 s, ZERO data races** (`host/store` **17.1 s green** against **FAIL + hang at 124.7 s** on go1.26.4, same worktree, minutes apart) — so *"just add `-race`"* was correct all along and for the wrong reason: the blocker was never the race detector. **FOUR OF MY OWN INSTRUMENTS OR CLAIMS FAILED, AND THREE WERE CAUGHT BY CONTROLS I RAN ON PURPOSE.** (i) I ran `GOARCH=amd64 go build && ./amd64 | sed …`, got **empty output with rc=0**, and was about to record *"amd64 is unaffected"* — the binary had never run (`bad CPU type in executable`; no Rosetta on this rig). That is Gate-2 rule 3a's vacuous-pass class, live, in my own hands, one iteration after the sibling loop paid for the same thing; **amd64 scope stays UNDETERMINED** and AC6 settles it in CI. (ii) My first full-repo `-race` sweep reported two FAILs I was about to attribute to `-race`; a no-`-race` control showed the broker one fails **either way** with `AILANG_BIN must name the pinned released interpreter` — I had simply not exported it, and that is the **M6/B1 anti-false-green guard working exactly as designed**. Both FAILs vanished once it was set. (iii) A verbatim test-only copy of the miscompiled loop **passed** under `-race`, because my diagnostic `t.Logf` calls perturbed the optimizer away — the heisenbug signature that pointed at the compiler in the first place. (iv) An inherited inference **refuted by reading the code**: iter-38 recorded that `Rows[0].Ref` was *"empty alongside `Field`, consistent with the `fields` array reading as all-zero … a memory-corruption signature"*, but `ScanUnreadableLog` **never assigns `Ref`** on that path, so it is empty in every run — the strongest stated evidence for the corruption reading rested on a field that is empty by construction. **THE QUORUM BLOCKED TWICE AND WAS RIGHT BOTH TIMES, AND THREE OF ITS FOUR OBJECTIONS WERE AGAINST TEXT I HAD JUST WRITTEN TO SATISFY AN EARLIER OBJECTION.** r1 `gpt5-6-sol`: my `verify_go.sh` design exported `GOTOOLCHAIN=go1.25.6` **before** asserting the version, so a hostile `GOTOOLCHAIN=go1.26.4` was overridden and **AC2 could never fail** — *a gate that cannot fail, reintroduced inside the remedy for a gate that cannot fail.* r1 `gemini-3-1-pro`: the claim that a `toolchain` directive cannot pin was load-bearing and **unlogged** — valid, now **P12** — but its factual counter-claim (*"the toolchain directive explicitly forces the exact version, overriding local toolchains"*) is **REFUTED by measurement**: with `toolchain go1.25.6` and go1.26.4 local, `go version` reports 1.26.4, the program reproduces, and **`go version -m` stamps the binary `go1.26.4`**, with positive and negative controls in the same run. Had I deferred to the reviewer, **the pin would have been decorative while the doc claimed it was pinned**. r2 `gpt5-6-sol`: adding P12 made my own exposure paragraph **self-contradictory** — a `go` directive is a floor, so config cannot prove what historical builds resolved; split into **P8a** (config, code read) / **P8b** (**measured** `go1.26.4 linux/amd64` from CI run `30483249118`'s step log — I read the log rather than record it undetermined as the reviewer suggested) / **P8c** (**NOT CLAIMED**). r2 `gemini-3-1-pro`: my softened assign-if-unset form was **still a silent fallback** and contradicted the sentence immediately after it (*"a verifier that also silently sets the thing it verifies is not a verifier"*) — accepted in full; the script now **sets nothing**, reads `go env GOVERSION`, and fails loudly. Both r2 objections carried concrete reviewer-authored fixes and disputed no part of the **direction**, so the **narrow-refinement carve-out** applied for the bounded 2nd revision. **The lesson: a revision is not a smaller change than an original, and deserves the same adversarial read.** **PARKED FOR MARK, and nothing about the build changed this iteration:** **OD-1** (lower `go.mod` 1.26.4 → 1.25.6 — ratification-class; recommended **YES**: the newest stable Go miscompiles a shape in landed durability code, there is no fixed release to move to, and the only dependency constraint `modernc.org/sqlite` needs just 1.25.0) and **OD-2** (file the 52-line reproducer upstream at **`golang/go`** — a public post to a third-party project, which is outside anything this loop is authorised to do; recommended **YES**, repro ready to paste). Also measured: `go.mod` **cannot** pin an older toolchain at all — both directives are floors, only `GOTOOLCHAIN` selects exactly — so the pin must live in CI's env and the developer's, and `verify_go.sh`'s job is to **verify, not to set**. Cross-mission alert sent to the V1 loop: `sunholo-data/ailang` is a Go project on this same rig and may share the exposure. Gate-2 discipline held: the 4e row's claims were **re-measured first-party at HEAD `8ed04c0`** rather than inherited, the already-landed check ran against a fresh origin **with a known-positive control**, and the `-race`-absent-from-CI claim was re-verified with a known-positive control in the same call. The reproduction is committed as a **re-runnable fixture with its own known-positive controls** (`design_docs/verification/w-race-gate-blindspot/run.sh`) that **exits non-zero rather than reporting a clean result** if it cannot see the defect it exists to see — because prose decays and an executable artifact does not, and because if Go fixes this upstream the next reader must be told, not reassured. **Queue: 4e mechanism DONE, remediation parked; 4d `w-ddl-gate-teeth` and 4f `w-bench-load-confound` remain at the head.** New carry-forwards **CF-K-1** (RG.A: the pin + canary + `-race` leg, blocked on OD-1) and **CF-K-2** (`bench/BASELINE.md` must record the toolchain as a condition of comparability — a toolchain change invalidates every number in it, which folds into item 4f). 4 stamps → newest 3 kept (iter-40, iter-39, iter-38); the positionally-4th (iter-37) archived this Gate 4.

(ii) **OQ-5's premise was FALSE, and OQ-5 is a STOP instruction**: it justifies its default by *"the caller-supplied logical clock the broker **already threads for records**"*, but `LogicalTime` appears **zero times** in `host/broker` production code and `EffectRecord` has no such field — unexamined, the executor would have been CORRECT to stop and the milestone would have died at checkpoint one. The conclusion survives its false premise: `EffectRequest.Now` is caller-supplied, in scope in `Invoke`, already feeds `requestHash` and capability expiry, and `time.Now` appears nowhere in `host/broker` production code while `EffectRequest` is constructed **only** in `_test.go` files — so `req.Now` is a logical value by construction. Resolved and handed down **with its evidence**. (iii) **The plan's own verify command prescribes a known-red gate** (`-race` over `host/store`). **THE ITERATION'S SPINE, AS FIRST WRITTEN — THEN RETRACTED IN THE SAME GATE 5 (see the correction at the end of this stamp): `git checkout <file>` IS NOT A MUTATION REVERT — IT IS A MILESTONE DELETION WEARING ONE.** Reproducing the headline gate myself (`MUT-INTENT-AFTER-DISPATCH`, **2 red / 99 green**, message `state "not-started" … want indeterminate`, matching the executor exactly), the revert step `git checkout host/broker/broker.go` restored from **HEAD** — and every sprint in this mission is delivered as an **UNCOMMITTED** worktree diff, because the codex sandbox cannot commit. So it silently discarded the milestone's entire `broker.go` and left a **green suite running on `origin/dev`'s code**. Caught **only** by the sha256 baseline the mutation discipline already demanded for a different reason (`99f3568…` → `6bc8514…`); recovered from the executor's own logged `git diff` and proven restored by **two independent hashes** — `git hash-object` returning the diff's recorded post-image `1fb60d78…` and sha256 returning `99f3568…`. **The rule: when the baseline is uncommitted work, the only safe revert is a copy taken before the mutation; the sha256 is what tells you which of the two you actually did.** This extends the iter-35 mutation-as-instrument discipline to its **other end** — validity covers applying a mutation AND undoing it. **A SECOND INSTRUMENT OF MY OWN CAME BACK CLEAN BECAUSE IT COULD NOT HAVE COME BACK DIRTY**: I reported "ZERO skips" from `go test ./...` piped through `grep -c -- "--- SKIP"`, but `--- SKIP` lines print **only under `-v`** — the zero was structural, not observed. Re-run with `-v`: **2 skips**, both pre-existing subprocess-helper guards (`crash_test.go:68`, `writer_lock_test.go:66`), benign — but "zero skips" has been treated as evidence here since B1. Same family as iter-37's glob-mangled `--include=*.go` and the zsh-empty `${PIPESTATUS[0]}`. **ONE MUTATION NAME, TWO FORMS, TWO ANSWERS — AND THIS TIME THE JUDGE HAD THE CONFOUNDED ONE.** Executor: `MUT-OUTCOME-BEFORE-RECORD` **2 red / 99 green**; judge, independently: **18 red**, every success path failing `refuses to persist an invalid ref ""`, concluding the form needs fault injection. Reading both applied patches settles it — the executor recomputed `recordObject(rec).Hash` so the outcome carried a **valid** ref and the mutation isolated the **ordering**; the judge passed the still-zero `recordRef`, so the store's own ref validator fired first and swamped the signal. **The executor's form is faithful, the plan's prediction stands, and CF-MJB-6 is REFUTED.** Fourth "one NAME two FORMS" instance (after `MUT-BENCH-DROP` iter-34, `MUT-PENDING-UNBOUNDED` iter-35) and the first where the **judge** ran the coarser form — a mutation's prose "form:" line does not determine the instrument; only the applied diff does. **THE JUDGE'S FINDING WAS REPRODUCED BEFORE BEING ACCEPTED, AND IT WAS UNDER-STATED.** CF-MJB-1 (filed non-blocking) says `recover.go`'s comment omits the Decision-5 residual. Measured: the doc's freeze block reads *"**One residual stays open and is stated, not hidden** … **No claim … may state or imply that every crash ambiguity is eliminated**"*, and the milestone had **deleted** the production comment carrying exactly that statement, replacing it with an unqualified *"Every commit and dispatched effect is therefore crash-detectable"* — the honest-claim discipline reversed in the one file a reader of `Recover` opens. **Fixed IN-PR, not carried**, with the full gate sweep re-run after. **THE EXECUTOR REFUTED ITS OWN PLAN, UNPROMPTED, AND WAS RIGHT**: `MUT-ORDINAL-ZERO-RESUME` was predicted at "1 red / ≥12 green, EVERY fresh-episode test green" and measured **11 red / 90 green**, because a fresh episode's *second* dispatch also collides with a constant ordinal 0 — mechanism held, discrimination claim too narrow; judge reproduced 11. **TWO DEVIATIONS THE EXECUTOR DID NOT REPORT**, both judge-confirmed, both non-blocking: **PD1 was explicitly violated** (a broker-side empty-episode guard was added where PD1 says "do NOT", and `broker_test.go:252` pins its exact string, so the store-layer guard is unreachable *through the broker* — it survives only because `journal_test.go:446` pins it directly); and **the capability ledger changed semantics unpinned** (the debit moved after the intent append, so the `no handler registered` path no longer debits — and `"no handler registered"` has **zero** tests, so neither the old nor the new behaviour was ever pinned). **THE CI GREEN WAS VERIFIED, NOT READ**: both jobs reported success in **36 s** and **8 s**, implausibly fast and exactly the shape of a gate that did not run — the step logs show all 11 modules ai-checked with `✓ 4/4 identities` and `✓ 14 named tests`, and `go build` + `go test ./... -count=1` with per-package `ok`; the Linux runner is simply ~3× the mac. Every gate re-run **OUTSIDE** the codex sandbox (the executor correctly labelled its own `verify_go.sh` UNINFORMATIVE UNDER SANDBOX with the verbatim bind error): `go build`/`go vet ./...`/`gofmt` clean, `go test ./... -count=1 -v` **rc=0, 180 PASS / 0 FAIL / 2 pre-existing SKIP across 10 packages**, `verify_go.sh` rc=0, `verify_ail.sh` rc=0 (4/4, 11 modules, 14 tests), broker-only `-race` rc=0, both forbidden-path assertions rc=0 **with a known-positive control**. **Queue: 4c [IN-SPRINT] — MJ.A + MJ.B LANDED, MJ.C is the last milestone**; **4d `w-ddl-gate-teeth`** and **4e `w-race-gate-blindspot`** still queued, and 4e's row is re-measured this iteration (the sibling package `host/broker` under `-race` is **GREEN in 4.095 s**, so the defect is `host/store`-local). New **CF-MJB-1…CF-MJB-6** (CF-MJB-1 DONE, CF-MJB-6 REFUTED); **CF-MJA-4/CF-MJA-5 were MJ.B-owned and are NOT discharged — they roll to MJ.C, stated rather than dropped**. Designer **not fired**; rotation pointer unchanged at `claude:claude-fable-5`. **THE RETRACTION, AT THE SAME VOLUME (Gate 5, after the above had already shipped to the log, this stamp and the public report): THE `git checkout` FINDING IS NOT NEW — IT IS A RECURRENCE, AND THE RECURRENCE IS THE BETTER FINDING.** This charter already forbade it **twice** — the mutation protocol's step 5 (*"revert by `cp` from a backup taken first — never `git checkout --` on a file carrying uncommitted work (iter-34 destroyed an executor's work that way)"*) and the M3.C paragraph concluding *"the instrument must not be able to delete the thing it measures"*. Same idiom, same class of file, **four iterations later**. So: **a written rule is not a control.** What caught it at iter-34 and again here was the **sha256**, not the prose — the hash pair is the control, the prose is a reminder, and a mutation record without both printed values is unverified. A rule that must be recalled at the moment of use will eventually not be, so the durable form belongs in the **command** (take the backup in the same call that applies the mutation) rather than in a document. And iter-34 had also already recorded the check that would have caught this over-claim — *"before calling anything an Nth instance of a known class, grep the repo's own rows"* — which I ran one gate too late. The charter bullet is reframed from a new rule to a recurrence; **the Gate-4 record and the GitHub report both shipped "new finding" first and are corrected in place.** 4 stamps → newest 3 kept (iter-38, iter-37, iter-36); the positionally-4th (iter-35) archived this Gate 4.

## STATUS 2026-07-29 (iteration 36) — **`w-effect-journal` (item 4c) NEW-DOC LANDED + QUORUM-CLEARED** (PR #25 → squash `fe582b5`, dev CI green **both jobs, SHA-addressed on the merge commit**; designer `claude:claude-fable-5` on the rotation lane, quota bucket; quorum metered **$0.164** against the $5 ceiling; **no sprint routed — this was a design iteration**). **THE ITERATION'S SPINE: THE QUEUE ROW'S OWN COSTING CLAIM WAS FALSE, AND THE GATE IT CITED AS PROOF IS INERT.** The M3 planner costed this item as *"Cheaper than assumed — **no schema change** (the commit shape lives in the payload codec, not the DDL, so AC13's `sqlite_master` gate stays green)"* — a claim its own `plan.json` labels **"Planner's measured note"**, restated in the charter queue row and carried into the ratification record. The premise is TRUE (the eight commit refs do live in the payload codec, `journal.go:104-143`); the **conclusion does not follow**, because the journal table's *kind vocabulary* and its *cardinality* live in the DDL. Measured first-party at `0f2afad`: **P1** `INSERT … kind='effect-intent'` → `CHECK constraint failed: kind IN ('intent','outcome')`; **P2** a second `kind='intent'` for the same `invocation_id` → `UNIQUE constraint failed: journal.invocation_id, journal.kind`; **P3, the sharp one** — a widened CHECK re-applied to an **existing** store returns **rc=0, no error**, `sqlite_master` shows the DDL **unchanged**, and the new-kind insert then fails on the OLD constraint. With **zero** migration machinery anywhere in `host/` (no `user_version`, no `ALTER TABLE`), **any DDL change in this repo ships FAIL-OPEN** — new stores get the new schema, every existing store silently keeps the old one, nothing detects the disagreement. *A schema change that was never applied is indistinguishable from one that was* — **iteration 35's spine, one layer down, at the schema instead of the mutation.** **AND THE CITED GATE HAS NO TEETH THERE — three compiling mutations, reverted byte-identical**: `MUT-JOURNAL-DDL-WIDEN` leaves `go test ./...` **rc=0 across all 10 packages** (*nothing* in the repo guards the journal DDL); the gate's OWN `MUT-DDL-DRIFT` does red but — **read the MESSAGE, not the exit code** (the iter-34 rule paying off) — via the **sha256 source pin**, which `t.Fatalf`s before a database is built, so the `sqlite_master` comparison never runs; and `MUT-DDL-COMPARE-DEAD` (short-circuiting that comparison, both vars still used, `go vet` rc=0) leaves the whole package **GREEN**. The gate's real teeth are a source-text pin over the pre-journal *prefix* — genuine protection for pre-existing tables, delivered by a different mechanism than the one it is documented as having, and covering neither the journal table nor the upgrade path. **FIFTH instance of this mission's signature shape** (a gate no production change could fail) and the **first found at PICK time**, in a gate inherited from an item already COMPLETE and judged **zero-blocking** — exactly the exposure the iter-32 process fix names. Raised as its own queue row **4d `w-ddl-gate-teeth`**, not smuggled into 4c. **THE DESIGN**: Path A, DDL-free — per-effect synthetic IDs in a reserved `effect:<episodeID>:<ordinal>` namespace, two payload codecs, reusing the existing `kind` vocabulary; Path B (new kind labels) REJECTED on the fail-open evidence, since it would honestly cost a migration mechanism first (~2.5–3d, not ~1.5d). **TWO QUORUM ROUNDS, TWO COLLISIONS IN THE SAME FIELD.** r1 `gemini-3-1-pro` **reject**: the in-memory ordinal counter is never re-initialized after a crash, so a resumed broker's first dispatch collides with the durable `effect:<episodeID>:0` and **bricks the episode** — r0 had *frozen* the opposite claim ("collision-free by construction"), which is FALSE across a crash boundary and was corrected everywhere it was restated. `gpt5-6-sol` was **ABSENT — `budget`**, a pre-flight refusal at *"$0.1005 exceeds cap $0.1000"*, zero spend, degraded N−1 and named (never a silent pass). The r1 fix **declined the reviewer's own proposed variant with a reason**: `len(episode.History)` counts *records*, and an indeterminate effect is precisely an intent whose record was lost, so it still collides after exactly the AC7 crash. r2 (cap raised to $0.25 so the absentee could run; **both present**) `gemini-3-1-pro` **PASS**, `gpt5-6-sol` **reject** — the r1 fix is **not atomic**: derive-then-append are separate operations, so *"the resumption fix merely replaces the restart collision with a TOCTOU collision."* Applied **VERBATIM** under the charter's **narrow-refinement carve-out** (concrete reviewer-authored fix; determinism-only; direction endorsed — Path A, the journal shape, intent-before-dispatch and the ID namespace all accepted): one transactional `AppendNextEffectIntent(episodeID, intentWithoutID) (id, ordinal, err)` mints the ordinal **inside** the appending transaction, `AppendEffectIntent` and `NextEffectOrdinal` are both REMOVED, the broker holds **no ordinal state at all**, `MaxInt64` exhaustion gets a structured error, and the reviewer's two named tests land as **AC7b** with its evidence ask as **AC7c**; `gemini`'s `GetReceipt` namespace guard adopted too, **closing OD1**. Nothing was force-passed and no objection was overridden — each was *satisfied*. **V28 — HALF THE OBJECTION IS REFUTED BY LANDED CODE, AND THE FIX WAS APPLIED IN FULL ANYWAY**: two broker *processes* cannot share a store as writers (`store.Open`'s non-waiting exclusive lock → `*WriterAlreadyActive`, proven cross-process), but the in-process half is REAL — **zero mutexes** in `store.go`/`journal.go`, so two goroutines can interleave freely. *Narrowing a fix to the part of an objection you can refute is how a real defect survives a review.* **V29 — A PROCESS FINDING AGAINST MYSELF**: my own premise-row additions (V21–V25) pushed the doc to ~13,952 input tokens and tripped round 1's per-reviewer cap, costing that round the very reviewer that later found the TOCTOU — **enriching a doc degraded its own review**. Also: **my Gate-3b poll was a broken instrument of my own making** — `target=$(git rev-parse A || cd B && git rev-parse HEAD)` binds as `(A || cd) && HEAD`, so `$target` held TWO SHAs, every API call was malformed, and the loop printed blanks toward its deadline; the iter-24/iter-107 meta-rule ("use the shipped snippet verbatim; a hand-rolled variant is a new defect surface") caught in the act, third instance. Re-run with a single literal SHA: **both jobs `completed/success`** on PR head `de0fb59` and again on merge commit `fe582b5`. So (i) a **built-in `submit_feedback` tool is exposed under EVERY flag combination and no flag removes it** — and its own tool description, which I captured verbatim, routes to a `public-feedback` inbox with a **Pub/Sub notification**, i.e. a built-in *egress* tool no World session authorized, sitting inside a bar whose clause 2 demands zero cloud dependencies in the core and whose clause 3 demands that **no ambient-authority path exist from an agent to the outside world**; (ii) discovery is built from loaded module exports rather than a caller-supplied provider, and `--caps` / `--routes-only` / `--api-key-*` are all **process-wide**, so a static key and a process cap set cannot represent a session. My earlier finding that `--a2a` publishes the 8 embedded `std/io` exports (`writeBytes`, `exit`, `readLine`, …) as callable A2A **skills** turned out to be the *less* severe half: `--routes-only` does suppress those, which also means the older upstream `#145` is genuinely FIXED and this is not its regression. Two further first-party facts for whoever builds this: MCP HTTP at `/mcp/` replies **SSE-framed** (`event: message` + `data:`), not a plain JSON body; and module resolution is **cwd-sensitive** — serving `design_docs/sketches/` from the repo root fails `LDR001: module not found: sketches/worldtypes` while serving `sketches/` from `design_docs/` succeeds, so the charter's 2026-07-23 live-test premise row (which records no cwd) is insufficient as launch configuration. **CONSEQUENCE: reuse paths (a) and (b) are REJECTED ON EVIDENCE, and the design takes path (c)** — request a narrow public serving seam from the existing `internal/apiserver` (mount MCP-HTTP/A2A handlers on a caller-owned mux; resolve principal/session before discovery *or* invocation; caller supplies the exact visible descriptors; named invocations route back with the same session; MCP tools and A2A skills generated from that one set; **no built-in tool unless the caller supplies it**; upstream keeps codec ownership and SSE conformance). Path (b) — a sidecar per session — was rejected because it makes process lifetime the session model and still exposes `submit_feedback`; reverse-proxy filtering was rejected because it would make World parse and re-encode MCP/A2A, the exact reinvention DESIGN.md §3.7 forbids. **ROUTED UPSTREAM ON BOTH CHANNELS: `sunholo-data/ailang#498`** + `msg_20260728_015014_8e5a281e`, with the full stdio repro, the version pin, `#145` cited as the fixed predecessor, a narrower interim ask (a flag that suppresses `submit_feedback` + document that `--caps` gates execution not discovery), and the **cause stated as a labelled HYPOTHESIS** since upstream source was never inspected. **THE QUORUM EARNED ITS KEEP TWICE AGAIN.** Round 1 (both present, `metered=$0.0634`) **BLOCKED**: `gpt5-6-sol` — no bounded-wait contract anywhere on the projection path (no timeout source, maximum, context-propagation rule, cleanup rule, error mapping, acceptance test or mutation for a stalled resolver/registry/broker/verifier/client; "broker unavailability returns an error" is not a contract); `gemini-3-1-pro` — mounting SSE-framed MCP handlers on the worldd daemon ignores that a REST daemon's `ReadTimeout`/`WriteTimeout` **abruptly kill long-lived SSE streams**. **I ran gemini's own "verify" step, and it collapsed its two-branch fix to ONE branch**: gemini offered "use `http.ResponseController` **or** document that the daemon lacks global timeouts", and the second branch is **FALSE here** — VERIFIED BY ME at `host/daemon/daemon.go:409-414` wiring constants at `:77-91`: `ReadHeaderTimeout` 5s / `ReadTimeout` 30s / `WriteTimeout` 30s / `IdleTimeout` 120s, **the D7 bound-constant block that `w-worldd-m2` A2 ratified and FROZE**.
